DRAFT 3.1 is the orchestrator's text repairs of `scratch/layer_dag/wave2_spec/verify_draft3.md` (repairs 1 to 8: the Lambertian divisor's reduction named, L-L's ulp bound measured, `from_binding` takes the method's realizer table, `Wall` composing `WallAmplitudes` in 2.9, the cone negation quantified, the instrument citations, three S5 test files, the cycle instrument), and the S5 exception class decided. DRAFT 3 was the second amendment of 2026-10-22 night, which applies the orchestrator's decisions on the two rechecks of DRAFT 2 (`scratch/layer_dag/wave2_spec/recheck_{elegance,structure}.md`; section 9 decisions 45 to 57, each marked `orchestrator, DRAFT 3`) to DRAFT 2, the amendment that applied the thirteenth exchange of `boundary_law_ontology.md`, the two checks `scratch/layer_dag/wave2_spec/check_{elegance,structure}.md` and the orchestrator's decisions C1 to C7; section 9 decisions 28 to 44 record each change with its source, and every superseded statement is kept beside its `[REFUTED 2026-10-22]` marker. ## 1. The mathematics in brief, each part with the type that realizes it

Notation. A domain `D` has a finite set of boundary faces `F` (strings: `"xmin"`, `"xmax"`, `"ymin"`, ... the vocabulary of `orpheus/geometry/faces.py`, `[M]` `face_name`, `face_normal`, `face_opposite`, `FACE_NAMES`; no `FaceName` type exists in the tree, `[M]` 0 definitions by `_defs.py`, and none is minted). For a face `f` with outward unit normal `n_f` the outflow half-trace is `Gamma_+(f) = f x {Omega : Omega.n_f > 0}`, the inflow half-trace `Gamma_-(f)` its complement, both carrying the trace measure `|Omega.n_f| dOmega dA`. A transport equation on `D` is closed when `gamma_- psi` is given on every face.

**1.1 The face trace and the two ways to close it.** `gamma_- psi |_f` is determined by the solution in exactly two ways, and they are different objects.
- *The domain continues across `f`* (a deck transformation). `gamma_- psi|_f = G_g gamma_+ psi|_{f'}` with `G_g` the Koopman operator of an isometry `g` of the covering space carrying the partner face `f'` onto `f` (`f' = f` for a mirror). `G_g` is invertible, measure preserving and has no parameter beyond `g`: it has no amplitude, no source, nothing to scale. Realized by `geometry/deck.py` (section 2.1).
- *The domain ends at `f`* (a boundary condition). The exterior is modelled by an affine map on the face's own half-traces, `gamma_- psi|_f = R_f gamma_+ psi|_f + q_f`, with `R_f` a kernel `L2(Gamma_+) -> L2(Gamma_-)` and `q_f >= 0` a function on `Gamma_-`. Realized by `transport/boundary_conditions/` (section 2.4).

**1.2 The deck is a closed family, a theorem and not a choice.** The admissible `g` normalise the chart's continuous group `G_c` (the group of the coordinate system: translations of a slab, rotations of a cylinder or sphere), so the admissible decks are `W = N(G_c)/G_c`. `W` is trivial for the 1-D cylinder and sphere, so no 1-D curved face carries a deck: a reflecting curved wall is a response (`WallCondition.specular(1.0)`), never a deck. The non-identity isometries of `E^n` fall into finitely many conjugacy classes (Chasles): `E^1` has translation and reflection; `E^2` adds rotation and glide reflection; `E^3` adds screw motion and rotoreflection (six in all). Realized by `IsometryClass` (an `Enum`, six members), the minted deck types, and the deferral rows (section 2.1). `[M]` the geometry has the chart data this rests on: `CoordSystem.boundary_points` returns two points iff the system is Cartesian or `r_0 > 0` (`orpheus/geometry/coord.py:185`), `CoordSystem.angular_chart` gives the chart (`:168`).

**1.3 The condition as an affine trace map whose linear part is a tensor network (amended, thirteenth exchange).** Every kernel the methods share is written with `n_f` alone, so it carries no axis and no outward sign: the face supplies both. A shared exterior condition is the affine pair `(R, q)`, `gamma_- psi|_f = R gamma_+ psi|_f + q`, and the linear part `R` is a network of typed factor tensors on the rungs of one face's trace ladder: the outflow half-trace `Gamma_+`, the partial current `S`, the inflow half-trace `Gamma_-` (`orpheus/numerics/spaces/angular_trace_space.py`: `outflow_space`, `current_space`, `inflow_space`). The tensor-network reading of an operator is `docs/theory/foundations/operator_tensor_network.rst`; the factor tier and its two-test criterion (multiplicativity, then "is it a quotient?") are `orpheus/geometry/boundary/_factors.py:26-70`, and by that criterion a wall's mirror is constitutive (it belongs to `R`) and never a deck. The factors are:
- `P` (`MirrorPermutation`): `Gamma_+ -> Gamma_-`, `(P phi)(x, Omega) = phi(x, Omega - 2 (Omega.n_f) n_f)`, the ordinate permutation of the face's mirror plane.
- the split pair of the partial current: the retraction `Rt` (`CurrentRetraction`), `Gamma_+ -> S`, `Rt phi = sum over Omega.n_f > 0 of |Omega.n_f| w phi`, integration against the trace measure `|Omega.n_f| w` (`face_streaming_normal`, `orpheus/numerics/face_layout.py:298`); and its section `E` (`IsotropicSection`), `S -> Gamma_-`, `E J = J / M_-` with `M_- = sum over Omega.n_f < 0 of |Omega.n_f| w`, uniform in `Omega`. They are the project's canonical split pair, `Rt_in o E = id` on `Gamma_-` (in production as `AxisRetractionOperator` and `AxisSectionOperator`, `orpheus/numerics/axis_marginal.py:156,247`, "embedding" is not an operator name), where `Rt_in` is the retraction over the INFLOW rung.
- a scalar weight.

Two chains compose from `Gamma_+` to `Gamma_-`: the specular chain `P` and the diffuse chain `E o Rt`. The linear part is `R = s P + d (E o Rt)`, a point `(s, d)` of the convex cone `K = {s, d >= 0, s + d <= 1}`, and `q >= 0` is a function on `Gamma_-`. Vacuum is the zero network, `(0, 0)`; white is `d (E o Rt)`; a specular wall is `s P`; an inflow is a nonzero `q` over the zero network. Vacuum, white, the specular wall and the inflow are thin facade constructors (D1) over ONE type, `WallCondition` (section 2.4): `WallCondition.specular(0.0)`, `WallCondition.white(0.0)` and `WallCondition.vacuum()` are one value with one digest. `[REFUTED 2026-10-22]` DRAFT 1's decision 4 (two digests for `WallCondition.vacuum()` and `AlbedoBoundary(0.0, shape)`); source: the user, thirteenth exchange ("how can their physics be the same and magically the math be different?"); the fact it establishes: `R = 0` is one point of `K` whichever way it is reached.

The return fraction of the point is `rho = s + d`. It is exact by the split pair: `J^-(R phi) = s J^+(phi) + d Rt_in(E(Rt_out phi)) = (s + d) J^+(phi)`, using that `P` preserves the partial current (a measure-preserving permutation, certified at realization) and `Rt_in o E = id`. Equivalently the trace measure is a left eigenvector of `R`, `R^T (w|mu|)_- = rho (w|mu|)_+`. For the shared family this is a theorem of the construction (law L-A rider, section 3); a method-only kernel that is not built from this pair owes it as a gate. Adding walls is adding chains and sources. The chains of the shared family are a CLOSED sum, `Chain = SpecularChain | DiffuseChain`, read by exhaustive `match` with `assert_never`: a third chain is an edit of the sum (every match then reddens until it handles the new member), and it enters with its declared unit return fraction and the rider gate; a method-only kernel (the neutron-guide wall, an anisotropic SN kernel) is another condition type and never a chain (decision 48, `orchestrator, DRAFT 3`). The cone is a VALUE predicate (D2, decision 50): the result of any arithmetic on conditions (`+`, `-`, scalar `*`) is a `WallCondition` if and only if its coordinates lie in `K` (`s, d >= 0`, `s + d <= 1`, every inflow weight `>= 0`), and otherwise a displacement in the affine hull of `K` (a sensitivity, #529's parameter coordinates), a separate value that is never bindable; direct construction of a `WallCondition` outside `K` raises. The displacement value is `WallDisplacement` (the draft's `BoundaryDirection`, renamed because "direction" is the angular `Omega` in 57 files of `orpheus/`, `[M]` `git grep -l -w directions -- orpheus | wc -l`, so the draft's name reads as an ordinate; `WallDisplacement` has 0 definitions, `_amend_names.py`), and it is not bindable. Method-only responses stay their own types and are never terms of the shared family: zero flux (`ZeroFluxCondition`, `rho = -1`, outside `K`) and any anisotropic SN kernel. `WallAmplitudes(specular, diffuse)` (the reference's plain-data closed value, section 2.9) is exactly the coordinate pair `(s, d)` and maps one to one onto the linear part; the reference's `Wall(specular, diffuse)` (`orpheus/derivations/continuous/characteristic/walls.py:77`) is already this chart. Realized by `WallCondition`, `ResponseNetwork`, `WallDisplacement`.

**1.4 Face roles, tags, binding.** Each boundary face has exactly one role: glued (a deck) or facing the exterior (a tag). The role is the sum `FaceRole = DeckTransformation | int`. The integer is a colour of the true boundary, the exact twin of a material id (`orpheus/data/materials.py:108`: an `int`, not a `bool`): the geometry says which faces share an exterior model and never what the model is (eleventh exchange, ruling 1: tags are a declaration, not an observation). A *binding* is a map `beta : T -> BoundaryCondition` from the set `T` of tags the faces carry, supplied where the method is known (ninth exchange, ruling 2). Realized by `FaceRole`, `BoundaryConditions` (the twin of `Materials`), `Binding` and `bind`.

**1.5 The colouring stabiliser.** The problem's symmetry group is
`H = { h in Isom(D) : mat(h c) = mat(c) for every region c, role(h f) = h . role(f) . h^-1 for every face f }`
with `h . t = t` for a tag and conjugation for a deck. It is computed from the GEOMETRY's material runs and end roles, never from the mesh's cell edges, so one `(geometry, binding)` pair is admitted or refused for every mesh (ruling 1 of the eleventh exchange; attack math 3). Binding is lawful when, for every non-identity `h in H` that moves a face of tag `t`, `h_* beta(t) = beta(t)`. Every shared kernel is face-local, so the equation holds for it by a theorem; it can fail only for a condition whose content is stated in a frame the face does not supply (a prescribed inflow whose profile is written in laboratory directions). Realized by `colouring_symmetry` and `ColouringSymmetry` (geometry), `BoundaryCondition.face_local` and `.invariant_under` (transport), and the refusal in `bind`. "A face-local condition is invariant" is a one-dimensional theorem (section 2.4): in higher dimension a mirror fixing a face reverses its tangent frame.

**1.6 Realization is a partial map; admission is its domain.** For a method `M` with trace discretization `Pi_M`, `real_M : (declared, site, method space) -> B_f : Gamma_+,h(f') -> Gamma_-,h(f)` with `real_M(g) = G_{g,h}` for a deck and `real_M(beta) = R_{f,h}` for a condition, extended linearly over combinations. `q` realizes separately into the boundary-source channel (`bc-affine-source-channel`). Admission answers "is `real_M` defined at this value on this face, and how good is it". It uses the theory page's vocabulary (`docs/theory/foundations/boundary_conditions.rst`, `bc-method-realizability` and `bc-refusal-axes`, `[M]` read 2026-10-22):
- *tier 1* exact and faithful: `R = alpha I` for any `alpha` (a scalar commutes with every projection); `Realized(faithful=True)`;
- *tier 2* exact but not faithful: the projection identifies laws that differ upstairs (at P1, specular and Lambertian both give `J^- = alpha J^+`); `Realized(faithful=False)`;
- *tier 3* not exact: the law depends on structure the method does not represent; `Approximated(name, issue)`;
- *refused*, on exactly one of the three axes angular resolution, spatial/topological, state cone/sign, or on the fourth thing that is not an axis, plumbing (`q` or a registry entry with no theory behind it): `Refused(axis, reason, issue)`.

There are two levels. The *type level* is the front end's drop-down and the upper bound of the instance level. The *instance level* is per face and per value (CP admits a mirror on a slab's `xmin` only; SN admits at a hollow cylinder's inner face only the cavity its closure computes), plus the quadrature's equivariance, which `ordinate_pairing` certifies. Both levels read ONE table per method, the table the realizer dispatches through, so the query and the realization cannot disagree (`[M]` today they do: SN's string registry admits two tags while its realizer realizes six laws, `orpheus/sn/problem.py:192` against `orpheus/sn/boundary/realizer.py:800-1116`).

**1.7 The adjoint, stated once.**
- Every shared kernel is reciprocal in the trace metric `M = diag(w |mu|)`: the specular return is an involutive measure-preserving permutation `P`, so `M^-1 P^T M = P`; the Lambertian is `u (x) (w|mu|)/sum(w|mu|)`, self-adjoint under `M`. So the adjoint of a shared condition's realization is its realization on the reversed ordinates, and a forward solve and an adjoint solve share one binding.
- A deck's adjoint is its partner's deck: `G_g^# = G_{g^-1}`, which is the deck on the partner face (`pairing_of(d, f).inverse == pairing_of(d, partner(f))`).
- `q` is NOT adjointed. `[M]` `orpheus/sn/solver.py:2870` builds `AngularBoundarySourceSink.zeros(...)` in `solve_sn_adjoint_fixed_source` and never reads the declared `q`; the two other adjoint entries refuse a declared boundary source (`_refuse_a_boundary_source`, `:2294` and `:2654`). This is correct (`q` enters the reciprocity identity as `<q, |Omega.n| gamma_- psi^+>`) and has never been stated; law L-A (section 3) states and gates it.
- **The left-eigenvector rider (amended).** Reciprocity in `M` is a statement about `B^T`; the scalar `rho` a consumer reads (DSA, the corner swap, the leakage term, ERR-094) exists exactly when the trace measure is a left eigenvector of the realized map: `J^-(R phi) = rho J^+(phi)` for every `phi >= 0` iff `R^T (w|mu|)_- = rho (w|mu|)_+` (Perron-Frobenius for the sub-Markov kernel; `check_structure.md` frame 4). For the shared family it holds BY CONSTRUCTION, with one precondition that the shipped code does not meet in general: the section `E` divides by the INFLOW mass `M_-`, so that `Rt_in o E = id` (section 2.6 states the divisor rule and its measurement). The ratio witness "`J^-/J^+` of `inner` applied to one positive outflow equals `return_fraction`" cannot discriminate a wrong `rho` (a type that reports the one measured ratio passes), so the law is gated as the array identity `inner.apply_transpose(w|mu| on Gamma_-) == rho * (w|mu| on Gamma_+)` (`array_equal` for the specular image; for the diffuse image up to the split pair's own residual, "at most N/3 ulp", `axis_marginal.py:159-167`, `N` the slot count); a method-only kernel (an angle-dependent reflectivity, the neutron-guide wall) owes the same gate and, where it fails, has no scalar `return_fraction` and its consumers must compute `J^+ - J^-(R psi)` on the actual outflow.

## 2. The types

Conventions. `ContentIdentity` is `orpheus/values/content.py::ContentIdentity` (content-keyed equality and hash). "Parsed" means `__post_init__` refuses an illegal value by a `raise` (never a bare `assert`). `FrozenMapping` is `orpheus/values/content.py::FrozenMapping`. A class that mixes in `ContentIdentity` is `@dataclass(frozen=True, eq=False)` (a dataclass with `eq=True` shadows the mixin's dunders; `[M]` `tests/gates/values/test_content_identity.py:430-434`, `test_s5_9_every_content_class_takes_the_mixins_dunders`, reds on it) and needs a roster entry (section 5.0). A PART type that is only keyed through the encoder's frozen-dataclass rule (the factor and chain markers, `FaceFrameInflow`, `LabFrameInflow`, the admission members, `FaceSite`, `FacePairing`, `DeferredDeck`) is a plain `@dataclass(frozen=True)` and takes `content_identity=False` in its roster. Every module carries a maximal docstring (the documentation pass of WF2.5 lifts them). A "homonym check" row cites `_defs.py` (definitions) and `_homonyms.py` (word hits); the strongest claim wins, and a ceding name is renamed in section 7.

### 2.1 Geometry: `orpheus/geometry/deck.py` (new, unit L1)

```python
class DeckError(ValueError): ...                       # the geometry's refusal root; NOT a BoundaryError

class IsometryClass(Enum):                              # the six conjugacy classes of non-identity isometries (Chasles)
    TRANSLATION; REFLECTION; ROTATION; GLIDE_REFLECTION; SCREW_MOTION; ROTOREFLECTION
ISOMETRY_CLASSES_BY_DIMENSION: Mapping[int, frozenset[IsometryClass]]
    # {1: {TRANSLATION, REFLECTION}, 2: + {ROTATION, GLIDE_REFLECTION}, 3: + {SCREW_MOTION, ROTOREFLECTION}}

@dataclass(frozen=True) class MirrorDeck(ContentIdentity):       # no fields. isometry_class: ClassVar = REFLECTION
@dataclass(frozen=True) class TranslationDeck(ContentIdentity):  # no fields. isometry_class: ClassVar = TRANSLATION
DeckTransformation: TypeAlias = MirrorDeck | TranslationDeck
FaceRole: TypeAlias = DeckTransformation | int                   # an int is a boundary tag; bool refused

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DeferredDeck:                                      # a class named and not minted
    isometry_class: IsometryClass
    name: str                                            # the type it will have
    trigger: str                                         # the machinery that mints it (SCOPE-BOUNDARY)
DEFERRED_DECKS: Mapping[IsometryClass, DeferredDeck]
    # ROTATION      -> RotationDeck(partner: str)  trigger "the 2-D geometry value (ninth exchange, ruling 3)"
    # GLIDE_REFLECTION -> GlideDeck(partner: str)  trigger "the 2-D geometry value"
    # SCREW_MOTION  -> ScrewDeck(...)              trigger "a 3-D geometry value"
    # ROTOREFLECTION-> RotoreflectionDeck(...)     trigger "a 3-D geometry value"

@dataclass(frozen=True) class FacePairing:               # what a realizer reads of a deck, derived, never stored on the deck
    face: str; partner: str; motion: RigidMotion         # RigidMotion = orpheus/geometry/transformation.py:336
    # invariant (parsed): motion.linear orthogonal (RigidMotion's own); partner == face implies motion.linear is an involution
    @property is_self_paired -> bool                     # partner == face
    @property permutes_ordinates -> bool                 # not motion.is_translation   (single-sourced on RigidMotion, as SelfPairedDeck today)
    @property inverse -> FacePairing                     # (partner, face, motion^-1): the adjoint's pairing (law L-A)

def mirror_motion(axis: str, dimension: int) -> RigidMotion        # the ONE axis-letter -> reflection spelling (replaces _mirror_motion, geometry/boundary/_factors.py:597)
def translation_motion(face: str, dimension: int) -> RigidMotion   # the unit translation along +face_normal(face); `[M]` face_normal("xmin") == (0, -1), ("xmax") == (0, 1)
    # SIGNED BY THE FACE (structure check 3): g_f carries the partner onto the face, so g_xmin = -e_x and g_xmax = +e_x and the groupoid law g_partner = g_f^-1 closes. The vector's LENGTH is not content (a deck does not know the domain's extent); readers use motion.linear and is_translation only
def pairing_of(deck: DeckTransformation, face: str, *, dimension: int) -> FacePairing
    # ONE exhaustive match over DeckTransformation, ending `case _: assert_never(deck)`.
    # MirrorDeck: (face, face, the reflection whose plane is normal to face's axis); TranslationDeck: (face, face_opposite(face), unit translation along the axis)
def admitted_decks(coord: CoordSystem, axis: int) -> frozenset[type[DeckTransformation]]
    # CARTESIAN, any axis: {MirrorDeck, TranslationDeck}. CYLINDRICAL or SPHERICAL: axis 0 -> frozenset(), axis >= 1 -> {MirrorDeck, TranslationDeck}
def parse_face_role(role: object, where: str) -> FaceRole
    # a DeckTransformation member, or an int that is not a bool (no sign restriction: the twin of a material id); anything else TypeError naming `where`
def check_pairings_close(roles: Mapping[str, FaceRole], where: str) -> None
    # a TranslationDeck on f requires a TranslationDeck on face_opposite(f) (an involution); else DeckError
```

- **Why a deck has no field.** The face fixes the plane (mirror), the partner and the translation direction. `[M]` today the axis is a field (`ReflectiveBoundary.axis`, `PeriodicBoundary.axis`, `orpheus/geometry/boundary/reflective.py:103`, `periodic.py:95`) and `_law_from_tag` injects it from the face (`orpheus/transport/method.py:352,407`); a deck carrying no axis makes the wrong-axis declaration unspellable (the B3.4a and B3.4c class). Two mirror decks compare equal and generate the same group (`Z_2`) whichever face they sit on; the position of a mirror plane is not stored (a 1-D mirror acts on directions only: `SelfPairedDeck.mirror` refuses an affine part, because the offset is invisible to every discrete quantity). The group `(Lambda, P, v)` (Bieberbach) is deferred to its first reader (section 9).
- **Dispatch.** A closed sum type with exhaustive `match` (ruling 3 of the eleventh exchange). There is no deck registry. A method that forgets a deck fails pyright (`assert_never`), not a runtime `Refused(UNREGISTERED)`: the day `RotationDeck` is minted, every method's deck match fails until it decides (attack elegance B3, A4).
- **Why only two minted.** A rotation's partner is not derivable from the face (a quarter turn about a corner pairs `xmin` with `ymin` or `ymax`), a glide reflection and a screw carry a translation part, and a rotoreflection an order. Their fields are designed with the 2-D and 3-D geometry values (ninth exchange ruling 3: `GlideDeck` "minted with the 2-D geometry"). Minting them now would mint four types with guessed fields and five unreachable arms in every method (attack elegance B3). The classification stays complete because the deferred rows are keyed by the Enum.
- **A mirror's group and the torus.** `H` for a slab whose two faces carry `TranslationDeck` is the stabiliser of the fundamental domain; the orbifold is `S^1`, which has more reflections than `x -> a + b - x` (attack math 3: for cells `[0, 1, 4]` with materials `(A, B)` the circle has mirrors at 0.5 and 2.5). The value is therefore named `ColouringSymmetry` and its docstring says it is the fundamental domain's stabiliser until `DeckGroup` lands.
- **Name check.** `DeckTransformation`, `MirrorDeck`, `TranslationDeck`, `IsometryClass`, `DeckError`, `FaceRole`, `FacePairing`, `pairing_of`, `admitted_decks`, `parse_face_role`, `check_pairings_close`, `DeferredDeck`: 0 definitions in `orpheus/` and `tests/` (`_defs.py`); prose hits only in `.claude/` (`_homonyms.py`: `DeckTransformation` 3 files, `MirrorDeck` 2, `TranslationDeck` 2). `Refusal` (`orpheus/geometry/manifold.py:440`) and `Realization` (`orpheus/geometry/symmetry.py:1291`) are different classes; `DeckError` does not clash.

### 2.2 Geometry: the face roles of `StructuredGeometry`, and `orpheus/geometry/colouring.py` (new, unit L1; the field change is slice S1)

`orpheus/geometry/structured_geometry.py` (S1):

```python
class StructuredGeometry(ContentIdentity):

    face_roles: tuple[FaceRole, ...]            # was `boundaries: tuple[BC | BoundaryTraceLaw, ...]`; inner first, as boundary_points
    @property tags -> frozenset[int]            # the tags its faces carry

```
- **Invariants (parsed in `__post_init__`):** one role per boundary point (the count rule of `parse_boundary_laws`, renamed `parse_face_roles`); each entry through `parse_face_role`; every deck is in `admitted_decks(coord, 0)` for the radial first axis, else `DeckError("... W = N(G_c)/G_c is trivial on a 1-D radial chart: a reflecting curved wall is a response; tag the face and bind WallCondition.specular(1.0)")`; `check_pairings_close`.
- **Constructors keep their keyword names** (`slab(breakpoints, mat_ids, *, left, right)`, `cylinder(..., outer, inner=None)`, `sphere(...)`, `uniform_boundary(coord, breakpoints, mat_ids, law)`, `from_homogeneous(width, boundary)`, `from_thicknesses(..., boundaries)`); their values become `FaceRole`. The parameter names `law` and `boundary` keep their position and are renamed `role` in the docstrings only where the name misleads. `wigner_seitz_pin_cell` (today `boundaries=(BC("white"),)`, `structured_geometry.py:415`) declares `outer=0`; its docstring states that the Wigner-Seitz model binds tag 0 to `WallCondition.white()` and that the geometry cannot hold that (F1 of design B; there is no problem-level preset type, #529). `pwr_slab_half_cell` (`BC("reflective")` twice, `:448`) declares `left=MirrorDeck(), right=MirrorDeck()`: a fact about the lattice the half cell stands for.
- **`parse_boundary_law(s)` retire** (`structured_geometry.py:520,555`; the lazy `isinstance(law, BoundaryTraceLaw)` at `:547` is forbidden edge 1 of `item2_carve.md`). Geometry then names no response.

`orpheus/geometry/colouring.py` (L1):
```python
from orpheus.geometry.chart import COORDINATE_AGREEMENT_ULPS, coordinates_agree   # NOT defined here: the one geometry-wide coordinate-agreement convention (below, C11)

@dataclass(frozen=True) class FaceIsometry(ContentIdentity):
    motion: RigidMotion                       # affine: carries its position (x -> a + b - x)
    faces: FrozenMapping[str, str]            # the permutation it induces on the boundary faces
    @property is_identity -> bool

@dataclass(frozen=True) class ColouringSymmetry(ContentIdentity):
    elements: tuple[FaceIsometry, ...]        # identity first; for a 1-D slab one or two elements
@dataclass(frozen=True) class UncomputedSymmetries(ContentIdentity):
    carrier: str                              # e.g. "Mesh2D": the pessimistic typed answer where no group machinery exists
Symmetries: TypeAlias = ColouringSymmetry | UncomputedSymmetries

def colouring_symmetry(coord: CoordSystem, ends: tuple[float, float], breaks: Sequence[float],
                       ids: Sequence[int], role_low: FaceRole, role_high: FaceRole) -> ColouringSymmetry
    # `breaks` and `ids` are the RAW partition (a geometry's intervals and material ids, a mesh's regions, a MaterialMesh's cells and mat_map values). THIS function merges equal adjacent ids into runs, ONCE, at the definition site (C8): the three callers pass what they hold, so refinement independence is by construction and S1 gate 6 stays as its witness
```
- **Float rule (settled by `measure_float_rule.md`, `[M]` `_float_rule_probe.py`, `_p2.py`).** For a Cartesian interval with breakpoints `b_0 < ... < b_n` the reflection `m_i = (b_0 + b_n) - b_{n-i}` is in `H` iff (a) `coordinates_agree(m_i, b_i, scale=max(|b_0|, |b_n|))` for every `i` (the 64-ulp convention of the next bullet), (b) the material runs read the same reversed, compared EXACTLY (integer ids), and (c) `role_low == role_high` (the same tag, or the same deck type). Reasons, each measured: the exact comparison accepts 2 of 13 symmetric slabs (it refuses `(0, .1, .2, .3)`, `(0, 1/3, 2/3, 1)` and every `1e-8` scaling); `k * eps * (b - a)` accepts 12 of 13 and refuses the offset slab `(1e6, 1e6+.1, 1e6+.2, 1e6+.3)` (over 200000 exactly symmetric random slabs the mirror error reached 1184 `eps (b - a)` but only 3.0 ulp at the end-point magnitude); `64 * ulp(max |end|)` accepted 13 of 13 and 6 of 6 symmetric batteries and refused every asymmetric one, with the genuine ceiling 3.0 ulp (21 times below 64) and the battery's smallest real asymmetry 4.5e7 ulp above it. The tolerance errs toward a false symmetric only within 64 ulp (smallest accepted perturbation of `(0, 1, 2, 3)`: 5.1e-14); a false symmetric only matters for a non-face-local condition on a two-face tag, and the check it skips is a 64-ulp-small error. A declared symmetry is not an option: it is an input, and a wrong declaration is a false symmetric nothing checks.
- **One convention (X4, C11; amended).** Two coordinates of one geometry AGREE when they differ by at most 64 ulp at the scale of the quantity they are compared at: `coordinates_agree(a, b, *, scale) := abs(a - b) <= COORDINATE_AGREEMENT_ULPS * np.spacing(scale)` (elementwise over arrays), `COORDINATE_AGREEMENT_ULPS = 64.0`, defined once in `chart.py`, promoted from `chart._LEVEL_AGREEMENT` (`orpheus/geometry/chart.py:599`, read at `:696` as `abs(...) > _LEVEL_AGREEMENT * np.spacing(scale)` with `scale = max(r*^2, level^2)`, named in a docstring at `:672`; `[M]` 3 sites, `git grep -n _LEVEL_AGREEMENT`). Both uses already have this exact form, so the promotion is ONE convention with two scales and not two tolerances sharing a name (the elegance check, C11, asked which). The chord check's scale is `r*^2` (measured agreement 8 ulp typical, 75 to 100 in a recorded red, per the `chart.py:672` docstring); the colouring rule's scale is `max |end|` (measured ceiling 3.0 ulp, `measure_float_rule.md`). Both measurements travel beside the constant in its docstring, and re-measuring either re-runs both gates (L1 gate 9). A change that wants another tolerance at one use changes the convention or passes another scale; it never forks the constant. `orpheus/values/scalars.py` holds no tolerance and stays bit-exact. The orchestrator's decision C5(11) settles the question; the user did not rule on it.
- **Radial charts return the identity only** (`G_c` absorbs the rotational symmetry and `W` is trivial). A multi-axis carrier returns `UncomputedSymmetries("Mesh2D")` (`SCOPE-BOUNDARY[guard]`, machinery: the wallpaper-group search over a cell's isometries; trigger: the 2-D geometry value); the typed pessimistic answer replaces the optimistic `{e}` that attack elegance A6 forbids.
- **Why from raw partitions (C8).** `StructuredGeometry.symmetry` passes `(ends, breakpoints, mat_ids, roles)`; `Mesh1D.symmetry` its region breaks and region material ids; `MaterialMesh.symmetry` its cell edges and the `mat_map` values; all RAW. `colouring_symmetry` merges equal adjacent ids into runs, ONCE, at the definition site (DRAFT 1 had three producers of runs feeding one function and a gate pinning their agreement after the fact, elegance C8). For one slab the runs agree whatever the partition, so refining a mesh cannot change admission. `[M]` attack math 3: a palindromic slab `(0, 1, 2)`, materials `(0, 0)`, tag 1 on both faces, a lab-frame beam, with a non-uniform cell partition, is admitted by a mesh-derived group and refused by the run-derived one.
- **Name check.** `ColouringSymmetry`, `FaceIsometry`, `UncomputedSymmetries`, `Symmetries`, `colouring_symmetry`, `COORDINATE_AGREEMENT_ULPS`: 0 definitions, 0 word hits in `orpheus/` and `tests/`. (`SubgroupOfO3`-style symmetry names in `orpheus/geometry/symmetry.py` are point groups; `Symmetries` is a different word: `[M]` 0 definitions of `Symmetries`.)

- `Mesh1D.face_laws` and `Mesh2D.face_laws` become `face_roles: FaceRoles`; `Mesh1D.outer_law` becomes `outer_role`; `Mesh1D.symmetry` is added (calls `colouring_symmetry` with the mesh's region breaks, never its cell edges); `Mesh2D.symmetry` is `UncomputedSymmetries("Mesh2D")`.

The inner role is carried by the axis and by `FaceRoles` but is NOT in `face_labels(axes)` (the trace's inventory): the radial axis has no inner-surface trace (`SCOPE-BOUNDARY[guard]`, machinery: an inner-surface trace on `RadialAxisMesh`, #511). The binding still carries it, so the method's `admit` sees it (section 2.7).

The pole is not a face role (it is the chart's singular stratum).
